  "new_string": "def _sync_post_scan_without_policy(\n    *,\n    plan: dict[str, object],\n    state: state_mod.StateModel,\n) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Run post-scan sync steps that do not require subjective policy context.\"\"\"\n    dirty = False\n    if _apply_plan_reconciliation(plan, state, reconcile_plan_after_scan):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_unscored_and_log(plan, state):\n        dirty = True\n    return dirty\n\n\ndef _sync_post_scan_with_policy(\n    *,\n    plan: dict[str, object],\n    state: state_mod.StateModel,\n    target_strict: float,\n    policy,\n    cycle_just_completed: bool,\n) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Run post-scan sync steps that require policy/cycle context.\"\"\"\n    dirty = False\n    if _sync_stale_and_log(\n        plan,\n        state,\n        policy=policy,\n        cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed,\n    ):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_auto_clusters_and_log(\n        plan,\n        state,\n        target_strict=target_strict,\n        policy=policy,\n        cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed,\n    ):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_communicate_score_and_log(plan, state, policy=policy):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_create_plan_and_log(plan, state, policy=policy):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_triage_and_log(plan, state, policy=policy):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_plan_start_scores_and_log(plan, state):\n        dirty = True\n    if _sync_postflight_scan_completion_and_log(plan, state):\n        dirty = True\n    return dirty"
}

list(state.get(\"issues\", {}).keys())\n    if plan is None:\n        return all_ids\n\n    seen_ids: set[str] = set(all_ids)\n    for fid in plan.get(\"queue_order\", []):\n        if fid in for: {pattern!r}\", \"yellow\"))\n    print(colorize(\"  Did you mean:\", \"dim\"))\n    for match in suggestions[:3]:\n        print(colorize(f\"    {match}\", \"dim\"))\n    if tip:\n        print(colorize(f\"  Tip: {tip}\", \"dim\"))\n\n\ndef _hash_suffix_suggestions(all_ids: list[str], suffix: str) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Return ID suggestions for an 8-char hash suffix pattern.\"\"\"\n    return [fid for fid in all_ids if fid.endswith(f\"::{suffix}\") or fid == suffix]\n\n\ndef _slug_suggestions(all_ids: list[str], *, last_seg: str, slug: str) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Return ID suggestions for a slug/segment pattern.\"\"\"\n    suggestions: list[str] = []\n    for fid in all_ids:\n        if f\"::{last_seg}::\" in fid or fid.endswith(f\"::{last_seg}\"):\n            suggestions.append(fid)\n            continue\n        if slug and (f\"::{slug}::\" in fid or fid.endswith(f\"::{slug}\")):\n            suggestions.append(fid)\n    return suggestions\n\n\ndef _suggest_close_matches(state: dict, plan: dict | None, patterns: list[str]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print fuzzy match suggestions for patterns that resolved to zero issues.\"\"\"\n    all_ids = _all_known_issue_ids(state, plan)\n\n    for pattern in patterns:\n        segments = pattern.split(\"::\")\n        last_seg = segments[-1]\n\n        if _HEX8_RE.match(last_seg):\n            suggestions = _hash_suffix_suggestions(all_ids, last_seg)\n            _print_suggestions(\n                pattern,\n                suggestions,\n                tip=f\"match by hash suffix alone: {last_seg}\",\n            )\n            continue\n\n        slug = segments[-2] if len(segments) >= 2 else \"\"\n        _print_suggestions(\n            pattern,\n            _slug_suggestions(all_ids, last_seg=last_seg, slug=slug),\n        )",
  "new_string": "def _suggest_close_matches(state: dict, plan: dict | None, patterns: list[str]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print fuzzy match suggestions for patterns that resolved to zero issues.\"\"\"\n    # Collect all known issue IDs from state, queue order, and cluster membership\n    all_ids: list[str] = list(state.get(\"issues\", {}).keys())\n    if plan is not None:\n        seen_ids: set[str] = set(all_ids)\n        for fid in plan.get(\"queue_order\", []):\n            if fid in seen_ids:\n                continue\n            seen_ids.add(fid)\n            all_ids.append(fid)\n        for cluster in plan.get(\"clusters\", {}).values():\n            for fid in cluster.get(\"issue_ids\", []):\n                if fid in seen_ids:\n                    continue\n                seen_ids.add(fid)\n                all_ids.append(fid)\n\n    for pattern in patterns:\n        segments = pattern.split(\"::\")\n        last_seg = segments[-1]\n\n        if _HEX8_RE.match(last_seg):\n            suggestions = [\n                fid for fid in all_ids\n                if fid.endswith(f\"::{last_seg}\") or fid == last_seg\n            ]\n            tip = f\"match by hash suffix alone: {last_seg}\"\n        else:\n            slug = segments[-2] if len(segments) >= 2 else \"\"\n            suggestions = []\n            for fid in all_ids:\n                if f\"::{last_seg}::\" in fid or fid.endswith(f\"::{last_seg}\"):\n                    suggestions.append(fid)\n                    continue\n                if slug and (f\"::{slug}::\" in fid or fid.endswith(f\"::{slug}\")):\n                    suggestions.append(fid)\n            tip = None\n\n        if not suggestions:\n            continue\n        print(colorize(f\"  No match for: {pattern!r}\", \"yellow\"))\n        print(colorize(\"  Did you mean:\", \"dim\"))\n        for match in suggestions[:3]:\n            print(colorize(f\"    {match}\", \"dim\"))\n        if tip:\n            print(colorize(f\"  Tip: {tip}\", \"dim\"))"
}

+def _write_desloppify_cli_helper(run_dir: Path) -> Path:
+    """Create an exact CLI wrapper so codex subagents use this checkout + interpreter."""
+    package_root = Path(desloppify.__file__).resolve().parent.parent
+    script_path = run_dir / "run_desloppify.sh"
+    script = (
+        "#!/bin/sh\n"
+        f"export PYTHONPATH={shlex.quote(str(package_root))}${{PYTHONPATH:+:$PYTHONPATH}}\n"
+        f"exec {shlex.quote(sys.executable)} -m desloppify.cli \"$@\"\n"
+    )
+    safe_write_text(script_path, script)
+    os.chmod(script_path, 0o755)
+    return script_path
+
+
+def _read_stage_output(output_file: Path) -> str:
+    """Return stripped stage output text, or an empty string when unreadable."""
+    try:
+        return output_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
+    except OSError:
+        return ""
+
+
+def _preflight_stage(
+    *,
+    stage: str,
+    plan: dict,
+    si,
+    append_run_log,
+) -> tuple[bool, str | None]:
+    """Fail fast when a requested stage has invalid upstream prerequisites."""
+    if stage != "organize":
+        return True, None
+    reflect_report = str(
+        plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
+        .get("triage_stages", {})
+        .get("reflect", {})
+        .get("report", "")
+    )
+    accounting_ok, _cited, missing_ids, duplicate_ids = _validate_reflect_issue_accounting(
+        report=reflect_report,
+        valid_ids=set(getattr(si, "open_issues", {}).keys()),
+    )
+    if accounting_ok:
+        return True, None
+    reason_parts: list[str] = []
+    if missing_ids:
+        reason_parts.append(f"missing={len(missing_ids)}")
+    if duplicate_ids:
+        reason_parts.append(f"duplicates={len(duplicate_ids)}")
+    reason = "reflect_accounting_invalid"
+    if reason_parts:
+        reason = f"{reason}({' '.join(reason_parts)})"
+    append_run_log(f"stage-preflight-failed stage={stage} reason={reason}")
+    return False, reason
+
+

   632→+def _analyze_reflect_issue_accounting(
   633→+    *,
   634→+    report: str,
   635→+    valid_ids: set[str],
   636→+) -> tuple[set[str], list[str], list[str]]:
   637→+    """Return cited, missing, and duplicate issue IDs referenced by reflect."""
   638→+    cited = extract_issue_citations(report, valid_ids)
   639→+    short_id_map: dict[str, str] = {}
   640→+    for issue_id in valid_ids:
   641→+        short_id = issue_id.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
   642→+        if re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", short_id):
   643→+            short_id_map.setdefault(short_id, issue_id)
   644→+    short_hits = Counter(
   645→+        short_id_map[token]
   646→+        for token in re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", report)
   647→+        if token in short_id_map
   648→+    )
   649→+    duplicates = sorted(issue_id for issue_id, count in short_hits.items() if count > 1)
   650→+    missing = sorted(valid_ids - cited)
   651→+    return cited, missing, duplicates
   652→+
   653→+
   654→+def _validate_reflect_issue_accounting(
   655→+    *,
   656→+    report: str,
   657→+    valid_ids: set[str],
   658→+) -> tuple[bool, set[str], list[str], list[str]]:
   659→+    """Ensure the reflect blueprint accounts for every open review issue exactly once."""
   660→+    cited, missing, duplicates = _analyze_reflect_issue_accounting(
   661→+        report=report,
   662→+        valid_ids=valid_ids,
   663→+    )
   664→+    if not missing and not duplicates:
   665→+        return True, cited, missing, duplicates
   666→+    print(
   667→+        colorize(
   668→+            "  Reflect report must account for every open review issue exactly once.",
   669→+            "red",
   670→+        )
   671→+    )
   672→+    if missing:
   673→+        missing_short = ", ".join(issue_id.rsplit("::", 1)[-1] for issue_id in missing[:10])
   674→+        print(colorize(f"    Missing: {missing_short}", "yellow"))
   675→+    if duplicates:
   676→+        duplicate_short = ", ".join(
   677→+            issue_id.rsplit("::", 1)[-1] for issue_id in duplicates[:10]
   678→+        )
   679→+        print(colorize(f"    Duplicated: {duplicate_short}", "yellow"))
   680→+    print(colorize("  Fix the reflect blueprint before running organize.", "dim"))
   681→+    return False, cited, missing, duplicates
   682→+

   756→+def _compact_issue_summary(triage_input: TriageInput) -> str:
   757→+    """Return a compact issue summary for later triage stages."""
   758→+    by_dim: Counter[str] = Counter()
   759→+    for issue in triage_input.open_issues.values():
   760→+        detail = issue.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(issue.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   761→+        by_dim[str(detail.get("dimension", "unknown"))] += 1
   762→+    dims = ", ".join(f"{name} ({count})" for name, count in sorted(by_dim.items()))
   763→+    parts = [
   764→+        "## Issue Summary",
   765→+        f"Open review issues: {len(triage_input.open_issues)}",
   766→+    ]
   767→+    if dims:
   768→+        parts.append(f"Open dimensions: {dims}")
   769→+    if triage_input.new_since_last:
   770→+        parts.append(f"New since last triage: {len(triage_input.new_since_last)}")
   771→+    if triage_input.resolved_since_last:
   772→+        parts.append(f"Resolved since last triage: {len(triage_input.resolved_since_last)}")
   773→+    return "\n".join(parts)
   774→+
   775→+
   776→+def _issue_context_for_stage(stage: str, triage_input: TriageInput) -> str:
   777→+    """Return the amount of issue context appropriate for a stage."""
   778→+    if stage in {"observe", "reflect"}:
   779→+        parts = ["## Issue Data\n\n" + build_triage_prompt(triage_input)]
   780→+        if stage == "reflect":
   781→+            short_ids = sorted(
   782→+                issue_id.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
   783→+                for issue_id in triage_input.open_issues
   784→+            )
   785→+            parts.append(
   786→+                "## Required Issue Hashes\n"
   787→+                f"Total open review issues: {len(short_ids)}\n"
   788→+                "Every one of these hashes must appear exactly once in your cluster/skip blueprint.\n"
   789→+                "Do not repeat hashes outside that blueprint.\n"
   790→+                + ", ".join(short_ids)
   791→+            )
   792→+        return "\n\n".join(parts)
   793→+    return _compact_issue_summary(triage_input)

index e761a1a..9e1038c 100644
--- a/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync_workflow.py
+++ b/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync_workflow.py
@@ -123,33 +123,92 @@ def sync_import_scores_needed(
     return _inject(plan, WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID)
 
 
+class ScoreSnapshot:
+    """Minimal score snapshot for rebaseline — avoids importing state.py."""
+
+    __slots__ = ("strict", "overall", "objective", "verified")
+
+    def __init__(
+        self,
+        *,
+        strict: float | None,
+        overall: float | None,
+        objective: float | None,
+        verified: float | None,
+    ) -> None:
+        self.strict = strict
+        self.overall = overall
+        self.objective = objective
+        self.verified = verified
+
+
 def sync_communicate_score_needed(
     plan: PlanModel,
     state: StateModel,
     *,
     policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
     scores_just_imported: bool = False,
+    current_scores: ScoreSnapshot | None = None,
 ) -> QueueSyncResult:
-    """Inject ``workflow::communicate-score`` when scores should be shown.
-
-    Injects when either:
-    - All initial subjective reviews are complete (no unscored dimensions), OR
-    - Scores were just imported (trusted/attested/override)
+    """Inject ``workflow::communicate-score`` and rebaseline scores.
 
-    And ``workflow::communicate-score`` is not already in the queue.
-    Appended to back of queue.  Never reorders existing items.
+    Injects when:
+    - All initial subjective reviews are complete (no unscored dims), OR
+      scores were just imported (trusted/attested/override)
+    - ``workflow::communicate-score`` is not already in the queue
+    - Score has not already been communicated this cycle
+      (``previous_plan_start_scores`` absent)
+
+    When injected and *current_scores* is provided, ``plan_start_scores``
+    is rebaselined to the current score so the score display unfreezes at
+    the new value.  The previous baseline is preserved in
+    ``previous_plan_start_scores`` so the communicate-score queue item can
+    show the old → new delta — and so mid-cycle scans know not to
+    re-inject.
     """
     ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
     order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
 
-    if WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID in order or WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID in order:
+    if WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID in order:
+        return _EMPTY()
+    # Already communicated this cycle — previous_plan_start_scores is set
+    # at injection time and cleared at cycle boundaries.
+    if "previous_plan_start_scores" in plan:
         return _EMPTY()
     if not scores_just_imported and not _no_unscored(state, policy):
         return _EMPTY()
+
+    if current_scores is not None:
+        _rebaseline_plan_start_scores(plan, current_scores)
+    # Set sentinel even when rebaseline was a no-op (no plan_start_scores
+    # to rebaseline) so mid-cycle scans don't re-inject.
+    if not plan.get("previous_plan_start_scores"):
+        plan["previous_plan_start_scores"] = {}
     return _inject(plan, WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID)
 
 
+def _rebaseline_plan_start_scores(
+    plan: PlanModel,
+    scores: ScoreSnapshot,
+) -> None:
+    """Snapshot the current score as the new baseline, preserving the old one."""
+    old_start = plan.get("plan_start_scores")
+    if not isinstance(old_start, dict) or not old_start:
+        return
+    if scores.strict is None:
+        return
+
+    plan["previous_plan_start_scores"] = dict(old_start)
+    plan["plan_start_scores"] = {
+        "strict": scores.strict,
+        "overall": scores.overall,
+        "objective": scores.objective,
+        "verified": scores.verified,
+    }
+
+
 __all__ = [
+    "ScoreSnapshot",
     "sync_communicate_score_needed",
     "sync_create_plan_needed",
     "sync_import_scores_needed",

+            stages_to_run=["organize"],

> AGENT

Good -- `TriageInput` is a dataclass with `open_issues` as an attribute. The `getattr(si, "open_issues", {})` call is correct for a dataclass. The type annotation `si: dict` on `_execute_stage` is misleading but `si` is actually a `TriageInput` at runtime. Not a bug but worth noting.

   118→def _seed_plan_start_scores(plan: dict[str, object], state: state_mod.StateModel) -> bool:
   119→    """Set plan_start_scores when beginning a new queue cycle."""
   120→    existing = plan.get("plan_start_scores")
   121→    if existing and not isinstance(existing, dict):
   122→        return False
   123→    # Seed when empty OR when it's the reset sentinel ({"reset": True})
   124→    if existing and not existing.get("reset"):
   125→        return False
   126→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   127→    if scores.strict is None:
   128→        return False
   129→    plan["plan_start_scores"] = {
   130→        "strict": scores.strict,
   131→        "overall": scores.overall,
   132→        "objective": scores.objective,
   133→        "verified": scores.verified,
   134→    }
   135→    # New cycle — clear the communicate-score sentinel so it can fire again.
   136→    plan.pop("previous_plan_start_scores", None)
   137→    # Record scan count at cycle start so gates can detect whether a new scan ran
   138→    plan["scan_count_at_plan_start"] = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   139→    return True
   140→
   141→
   142→def _has_objective_cycle(
   143→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   144→    plan: dict[str, object],
   145→) -> bool | None:
   146→    """Return True when objective queue work exists and a cycle baseline should freeze."""
   147→    try:
   148→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import (
   149→            plan_aware_queue_breakdown,
   150→        )
   151→
   152→        breakdown = plan_aware_queue_breakdown(state, plan)
   153→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
   154→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "compute queue breakdown for plan-start seeding", exc)
   155→        return None
   156→    return breakdown.objective_actionable > 0
   157→
   158→
   159→def _clear_plan_start_scores_if_queue_empty(
   160→    state: state_mod.StateModel, plan: dict[str, object]
   161→) -> bool:
   162→    """Clear plan-start score snapshot once the queue is fully drained."""
   163→    if not plan.get("plan_start_scores"):

   165→    # Don't clear while communicate-score is pending — the rebaseline just
   166→    # set plan_start_scores and the user hasn't seen the update yet.

> AGENT

There's a bug on line 459-465. The code does:
```python
dirty = _sync_post_scan_with_policy(...) or dirty
```

This means if `_sync_post_scan_without_policy` returned `True` (dirty=True), but `_sync_post_scan_with_policy` returns `True`, the result is correctly `True`. But if `_sync_post_scan_with_policy` returns `True` and the first was `False`, it's also correct. However, the issue is the ordering: `_sync_post_scan_with_policy(...) or dirty` will SHORT-CIRCUIT. If `_sync_post_scan_with_policy` returns `True`, it won't even evaluate `dirty`. That's fine for a boolean. Actually wait, `dirty` is already a value, not a function call. So `True or True` = `True`, `True or False` = `True`, `False or True` = `True`. This is actually correct. No bug here.

Wait, but compare to the original: the original code was `dirty = _sync_post_scan_without_policy(...)` then `dirty = _sync_post_scan_with_policy(...) or dirty`. Before the reorder, both were called. Now with the reorder, if `_sync_post_scan_with_policy` returns `False` and `dirty` from the first was `True`, the result is `True`. If the policy returns `True` and dirty was `False`, result is `True`. All correct.

   339→def _sync_create_plan_and_log(
   340→    plan: dict[str, object],
   341→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   342→    *,
   343→    policy,
   344→) -> bool:
   345→    create_plan_sync = sync_create_plan_needed(plan, state, policy=policy)
   346→    if not create_plan_sync.changes:
   347→        return False
   348→    if create_plan_sync.injected:
   349→        print(
   350→            colorize(
   351→                "  Plan: reviews complete — `workflow::create-plan` queued.",
   352→                "cyan",
   353→            )
   354→        )
   355→        append_log_entry(plan, "sync_create_plan", actor="system", detail={"injected": True})
   356→    return True
   357→
   358→
   359→def _sync_plan_start_scores_and_log(
```

> AGENT

The ordering concern: `_sync_communicate_score_and_log` (with rebaseline) runs before `_sync_plan_start_scores_and_log` (with seeding). But in `sync_communicate_score_needed`, the function checks `if WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID in order` (early exit) and `if "previous_plan_start_scores" in plan` (early exit). Then seeding runs after and would clear `previous_plan_start_scores` if it's a new cycle. The sequence is:
1. communicate-score: if conditions met, rebaselines `plan_start_scores` and sets `previous_plan_start_scores`
2. seed-start-scores: if `plan_start_scores` is empty/reset, seeds it and clears `previous_plan_start_scores`

This seems intentional: communicate runs within an active cycle, seeding happens at cycle boundaries. The `previous_plan_start_scores` sentinel prevents re-injection within the same cycle.

Now let me check for the `delta_str` bug I noticed earlier:

Line 229 in `synthetic_workflow.py`:
```python
delta_str = f" ({'+' if delta > 0 else ''}{delta:.1f})" if delta else ""
```

This is a **bug**: when `delta` is `0` (or `0.0`), the `if delta` condition is `False`, so `delta_str` becomes `""`. But `delta` being exactly 0.0 is a valid, non-None value. Previously with `plan_start` this same pattern existed though, so it's a pre-existing issue, not introduced by these commits. Let me verify by checking the old code:

Let me now check the `_sync_plan_start_scores_and_log` change more carefully. The `_has_objective_cycle` guard was removed. Previously the logic was:
1. Check if there's an objective cycle (queue has objective actionable items)
2. If yes, seed scores
3. If no, try to clear scores

Now the logic is:
1. Always try to seed scores
2. If seeding didn't happen (already seeded), try to clear scores

The `_seed_plan_start_scores` function itself checks if scores are already set (`if existing and not existing.get("reset"): return False`), so seeding will be a no-op if scores are already frozen. The key behavioral change is: previously, scores were NOT seeded if there were no objective actionable items. Now they're seeded whenever there are scores available and `plan_start_scores` is empty. This could be an intentional change to support the rebaseline flow, but it's a behavioral change that removed the "only seed when there's a queue cycle to track" gate. The test removal of `test_does_not_seed_when_only_postflight_work_remains` confirms this is intentional.

    60→    )
    61→

   104→
   105→def build_sense_check_structure_prompt(
   106→    *,
   107→    plan: dict,
   108→    repo_root: Path,
   109→) -> str:
   110→    """Build a structure-verification prompt for cross-cluster dependency checking."""
   111→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   112→
   113→    parts: list[str] = []
   114→    parts.append(
   115→        "You are checking cross-cluster dependencies for the entire triage plan.\n"
   116→        f"Repo root: {repo_root}"
   117→    )
   118→
   119→    parts.append(
   120→        "## Your job\n"
   121→        "Build a file-touch graph: for each cluster, which files do its steps reference?\n"
   122→        "Then check for unsafe relationships between clusters.\n"
   123→    )
   124→
   125→    parts.append(
   126→        "## What to check and fix\n"
   127→        "1. SHARED FILES: If cluster A and cluster B both have steps touching the same file,\n"
   128→        "   and neither depends on the other → report which dependency edge should be added.\n"
   129→        "2. MISSING CASCADE: If a step renames/removes a function or export, check whether\n"
   130→        "   any other file imports it. If those importers aren't covered by any step in any\n"
   131→        "   cluster → report the cascade step that should be added.\n"
   132→        "   Include the cluster name, affected importers, and issue hash in your report.\n"
   133→        "3. CIRCULAR DEPS: If adding a dependency would create a cycle, flag it in your report\n"
   134→        "   instead of adding it.\n"
   135→    )
   136→
   137→    parts.append(
   138→        "## What NOT to do\n"
   139→        "- Do NOT modify step detail text (the content subagent handles that)\n"
   140→        "- Do NOT change effort tags (the content subagent handles that)\n"
   141→        "- Do NOT remove steps or deduplicate (the content subagent handles that)\n"
   142→        "- Do NOT run any `desloppify` commands\n"
   143→        "- Do NOT debug or repair the CLI / environment\n"
   144→    )
   145→
   146→    # Include all clusters with their steps and dependencies
   147→    parts.append("## Clusters\n")
   148→    for name, c in sorted(clusters.items()):
   149→        if c.get("auto"):
   150→            continue
   151→        steps = c.get("action_steps", [])
   152→        deps = c.get("depends_on_clusters", [])
   153→        issues = c.get("issue_ids", [])
   154→        header = f"### {name} ({len(steps)} steps, {len(issues)} issues)"
   155→        if deps:
   156→            header += f"\n  depends_on: {', '.join(deps)}"
   157→        parts.append(header)
   158→        for i, step in enumerate(steps, 1):
   159→            title = step.get("title", str(step)) if isinstance(step, dict) else str(step)
   160→            detail = step.get("detail", "") if isinstance(step, dict) else ""
   161→            line = f"  {i}. {title}"
   162→            if detail:
   163→                line += f"\n     {detail[:200]}"
   164→            parts.append(line)
   165→
   166→    parts.append(
   167→        "\n## Output\n"
   168→        "Write a plain-text report of your findings. The orchestrator records the stage."
   169→    )
   170→
   171→    return "\n\n".join(parts)
   172→
   173→
   174→__all__ = [
   175→    "build_sense_check_content_prompt",
   176→    "build_sense_check_structure_prompt",
   177→]
   178→

This is a real bug. The new `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` check is now running during the reflect stage recording flow (via `_auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize`), and it rejects the reflect report because it doesn't contain the issue IDs. The existing tests use simple issue IDs like "r1", "r2" that aren't 8+ hex characters, so the hash matching in `_analyze_reflect_issue_accounting` can't find them.

> AGENT

The issue is clear. The `_auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize` function now validates that the reflect report accounts for every open issue. The test uses simple issue IDs like "r1" and "r2", and the reflect report is just a long generic text that doesn't mention these IDs. The `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` function uses `extract_issue_citations` which looks for 8+ hex char patterns -- and "r1", "r2" don't match that pattern.

This means the `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` function silently fails on non-standard issue IDs: the `short_id_map` only matches 8+ hex chars, and `extract_issue_citations` uses a regex for issue ID patterns. With IDs like "r1" and "r2", `valid_ids - cited` = all IDs, so they're all "missing" and the validation fails.

This is a real correctness issue. The new validation is too aggressive -- it blocks the reflect stage recording path when called from `_auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize`. The 9 failing tests all exercise the reflect or organize stage recording with simple test issue IDs.

> AGENT

This is the direct `cmd_stage_reflect` flow, and it also calls `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting`. With real issue IDs (8+ hex), this works. The bug manifests only when tests use simplified IDs.

However -- looking at it more carefully, the _auto_confirm path (called when organizing) checks the reflect report that was *already recorded*. If the reflect report was recorded successfully (it must have passed the validation in `cmd_stage_reflect`), then the `_auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize` validation is a redundant re-check. But if the reflect was recorded before this validation existed (e.g., from a previous version), it could legitimately fail.

The 9 test failures all use `cmd_plan_triage` which routes through `stage_flow_observe_reflect_organize.py`. The reflect recording path now has `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` as a gate. With test IDs like "r1", "r2" that aren't hex hashes, ALL ids are "missing" and validation fails.

This is the main bug in these commits. Let me summarize all findings.

---

Here is my review of both commits:

## Commit 1: `27a7c9e` "inline review batch execution paths"

### Correctness Issues

**None found.** The inlining is clean:
- `do_run_batches` was removed from `execution.py` and its logic moved into `orchestrator.py` (lines 341-381), calling `execution_phases` directly.
- `execution_summary.py`'s `build_run_summary_writer` was simplified from a `partial`-based factory to accept a pre-built `BatchRunSummaryConfig`, and `execution_phases.py` was updated to construct `BatchRunSummaryConfig` directly.
- All test assertions updated correctly.

### Dead code left behind

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution_summary.py` -- `build_run_summary_writer` is no longer called from production code. Its only import is from the test file `test_review_batch_execution_summary_direct.py`. The function in `execution_phases.py` now constructs the writer directly using `functools.partial(write_run_summary_impl, ...)`, bypassing `execution_summary.py` entirely. This module is dead code.

### helpers.py cleanup (question 4)

The `_resolve_import_load_config` wrapper was removed from `helpers.py` and its logic inlined into `load_import_issues_data`. The cleanup is correct. However, `_config_and_legacy_kwargs_conflict` and the dual interface on `load_import_issues_data` (accepting both `config=ImportLoadConfig(...)` and individual kwargs) remain. Per the backward-compat policy in CLAUDE.md, this could be cleaned up further since all callers now pass `config=ImportLoadConfig(...)`.

---

## Commit 2: `afc07f7` "reduce command complexity and harden triage/review flows"

### Bug: 9 test failures from `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` gate

**Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/_stage_validation.py` (lines 151-220) -- new `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` function
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stage_flow_observe_reflect_organize.py` (line 209) -- new validation call in `cmd_stage_reflect`

**Problem:** `_validate_reflect_issue_accounting` uses `extract_issue_citations` and a `re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", report)` pattern to match issue hashes. It then computes `missing = valid_ids - cited`. When issue IDs don't contain 8+ hex suffixes (e.g., test IDs like "r1", "r2"), zero IDs are ever cited, so ALL IDs are reported as "missing" and the validation fails.

This breaks the reflect stage recording path (`cmd_stage_reflect` and `_auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize`) for any plan with non-standard issue IDs. The 9 failing tests are:
- `test_triage_confirmation.py` (2 failures)
- `test_triage_fold_confirm.py` (2 failures)
- `test_triage_jump_back.py` (4 failures)
- `test_triage_logging.py` (1 failure)

### Potential issue: Type annotation mismatch

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py`

`_execute_stage` declares `si: dict` (line 227) but the actual runtime value is a `TriageInput` dataclass. The `_preflight_stage` function uses `getattr(si, "open_issues", {})` (line 205) which works correctly on a dataclass but would fail on a dict. The type annotation is misleading. Not a runtime bug since `si` is always a `TriageInput` at the call site (line 539).

### Behavioral change: `_sync_plan_start_scores_and_log` simplified

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py` (lines 359-368)

The `_has_objective_cycle` guard was removed. Previously, scores were only seeded when the queue had objective actionable items. Now scores are seeded unconditionally (whenever `plan_start_scores` is empty and scores exist). The test `test_does_not_seed_when_only_postflight_work_remains` was removed to match. This appears intentional to support the new rebaseline flow, but it changes when score baselines are established.

### Behavioral change: `sync_communicate_score_needed` removed from import path

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/plan_sync.py` (lines removed around 100-110)

The `sync_communicate_score_needed` call was removed from the post-import sync. The communicate-score injection now only happens during scan reconciliation. This means scores imported via `do_import` no longer trigger immediate communicate-score injection -- it waits until the next scan.

### Overzealous refactoring -- single-use helper remains

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py`

`_load_prior_reports_from_plan` (lines 62-70) is a new single-use helper called only from `run_codex_pipeline`. It's simple enough to inline. Similarly `_read_stage_output` (lines 178-184) is trivial and called once.

### Clean inlining (positive)

The `next/render.py` and `next/render_support.py` inlining is well-done -- `_render_plan_item_context`, `_normalize_issue_detail`, `_render_issue_detail_fields`, `_render_issue_code_snippet`, `_render_special_item`, `_render_standard_issue_header`, `_render_cluster_action_steps`, `_render_cluster_members`, and `_render_cluster_primary_action` were all correctly inlined into their single callers.

The `cluster_handlers.py` inlining (`_all_known_issue_ids`, `_print_suggestions`, `_hash_suffix_suggestions`, `_slug_suggestions` into `_suggest_close_matches`) is also correct.

### Test coverage

Tests are appropriate for the new features. The new tests in `test_triage_runner.py` and `test_triage_split_modules_direct.py` correctly exercise the new prompt modes, preflight validation, and CLI helper functionality. The only issue is the 9 pre-existing tests that were not updated to accommodate the new reflect validation gate.

> DEVELOPER

Fix a bug in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/_stage_validation.py` in the `_analyze_reflect_issue_accounting` function.

The problem: the function uses `extract_issue_citations` which only matches hex-hash-based IDs (`[a-z_]+::[a-f0-9]{8,}` or bare `[0-9a-f]{8,}` suffixes). When issue IDs don't contain hex hashes (e.g., test IDs like "r1", "r2"), zero IDs are matched and all are reported as "missing", causing 9 test failures.

The fix: In `_analyze_reflect_issue_accounting`, after the `extract_issue_citations` fallback path (the `else` branch after the Coverage Ledger check), also check for full valid_id strings appearing literally in the report text. This handles non-hex test IDs.

Replace lines ~183-191 (the fallback path after the Coverage Ledger check):

```python
    cited = extract_issue_citations(report, valid_ids)
    short_hits = Counter(
        short_id_map[token]
        for token in re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", report)
        if token in short_id_map
    )
    duplicates = sorted(issue_id for issue_id, count in short_hits.items() if count > 1)
    missing = sorted(valid_ids - cited)
    return cited, missing, duplicates
```

With:

```python
    cited = extract_issue_citations(report, valid_ids)
    # Also match valid IDs that appear literally in the report
    # (handles non-hex IDs like test fixtures).
    for issue_id in valid_ids:
        if issue_id in report:
            cited.add(issue_id)
    short_hits = Counter(
        short_id_map[token]
        for token in re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", report)
        if token in short_id_map
    )
    duplicates = sorted(issue_id for issue_id, count in short_hits.items() if count > 1)
    missing = sorted(valid_ids - cited)
    return cited, missing, duplicates
```

After making the fix, run: `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_confirmation.py desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_fold_confirm.py desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_jump_back.py desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_logging.py -q --tb=short`

If they still fail, read the failing tests to understand what report text they use and what issue IDs they have. The issue is that the test reports don't contain the issue IDs at all — in that case the tests need to include the issue IDs in their report text. Check each failing test's report string and the issue IDs in the state/plan.

   152→    dimension_notes = payload.get("dimension_notes")
   153→    if dimension_notes is None:
   154→        dimension_notes = {}
   155→    elif not isinstance(dimension_notes, dict):
   156→        errors.append("dimension_notes must be an object when provided")
   157→        dimension_notes = {}
   158→
   159→    policy = payload.get(ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY)
   160→    normalized_policy = (
   161→        policy if isinstance(policy, dict) else AssessmentImportPolicyModel().to_dict()
   162→    )
   163→    if errors:
   164→        return None, errors
   165→    return (
   166→        {
   167→            "issues": issues,
   168→            "assessments": assessments,
   169→            "reviewed_files": normalized_reviewed_files,
   170→            "review_scope": review_scope,
   171→            "provenance": provenance,
   172→            "dimension_notes": dimension_notes,
   173→            [REDACTED],
   174→        },
   175→        [],
   176→    )
   177→
   178→
   179→def _parse_and_validate_import(
   180→    import_file: str,
   181→    *,
   182→    config: ImportLoadConfig,
   183→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   184→    """Load, parse, and validate a review import file.
   185→
   186→    This helper performs filesystem I/O by reading ``import_file`` before
   187→    normalizing and validating the payload.
   188→
   189→    Returns ``(data, errors)`` where *data* is the normalized payload on
   190→    success, or ``None`` when errors prevent import.
   191→    """
   192→    options = config
   193→    issues_path = Path(import_file)
   194→    if not issues_path.exists():
   195→        return None, [f"file not found: {import_file}"]
   196→    try:
   197→        issues_data = json.loads(issues_path.read_text())
   198→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   199→        return None, [f"error reading issues: {exc}"]
   200→
   201→    if isinstance(issues_data, list):
   202→        issues_data = {"issues": issues_data}
   203→
   204→    if not isinstance(issues_data, dict):
   205→        return None, ["issues file must contain a JSON array or object"]
   206→
   207→    if "issues" not in issues_data:
   208→        return None, ["issues object must contain a 'issues' key"]
   209→    normalized_issues_data, shape_errors = _normalize_import_payload_shape(
   210→        issues_data
   211→    )
   212→    if shape_errors:
   213→        return None, shape_errors
   214→    if normalized_issues_data is None:
   215→        raise ValueError(
   216→            "normalized import payload missing after successful shape validation"
   217→        )
   218→
   219→    override_enabled, override_attest = resolve_override_context(
   220→        manual_override=options.manual_override,
   221→        manual_attest=options.manual_attest,
   222→    )
   223→    if options.attested_external and override_enabled:
   224→        return None, [
   225→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --manual-override"
   226→        ]
   227→    if options.attested_external and options.allow_partial:
   228→        return None, [
   229→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --allow-partial; "
   230→            "attested score imports require fully valid issues payloads"
   231→        ]
   232→    if override_enabled and options.allow_partial:
   233→        return None, [
   234→            "--manual-override cannot be combined with --allow-partial; "
   235→            "manual score imports require fully valid issues payloads"
   236→        ]
   237→    issues_data, policy_errors = apply_assessment_import_policy(
   238→        normalized_issues_data,
   239→        import_file=import_file,
   240→        attested_external=options.attested_external,
   241→        attested_attest=override_attest,
   242→        manual_override=override_enabled,
   243→        manual_attest=override_attest,
   244→        trusted_assessment_source=options.trusted_assessment_source,
   245→        trusted_assessment_label=options.trusted_assessment_label,
   246→    )
   247→    if policy_errors:
   248→        return None, policy_errors
   249→    if issues_data is None:
   250→        raise ValueError(
   251→            "assessment import policy returned no payload without reporting errors"
   252→        )
   253→
   254→    missing_feedback, missing_low_score_issues = _validate_assessment_feedback(
   255→        issues_data
   256→    )
   257→    if missing_low_score_issues:
   258→        if override_enabled:
   259→            if not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip():
   260→                return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   261→            return issues_data, []
   262→        return None, [
   263→            f"assessments below {LOW_SCORE_ISSUE_THRESHOLD:.1f} must include at "
   264→            "least one issue for that same dimension with a concrete suggestion. "
   265→            f"Missing: {', '.join(missing_low_score_issues)}"
   266→        ]
   267→
   268→    if missing_feedback:
   269→        if override_enabled:
   270→            if not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip():
   271→                return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   272→            return issues_data, []
   273→        return None, [
   274→            f"assessments below {ASSESSMENT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD:.1f} must include explicit feedback "
   275→            "(issue with same dimension and non-empty suggestion, or "
   276→            "dimension_notes evidence for that dimension). "
   277→            f"Missing: {', '.join(missing_feedback)}"
   278→        ]
   279→
   280→    schema_errors = _validate_holistic_issues_schema(
   281→        issues_data,
   282→        lang_name=options.lang_name,
   283→    )
   284→    if schema_errors and not options.allow_partial:
   285→        visible_errors = schema_errors[:10]
   286→        remaining = len(schema_errors) - len(visible_errors)
   287→        errors = [
   288→            "issues schema validation failed for holistic import. "
   289→            "Fix payload or rerun with --allow-partial to continue."
   290→        ]
   291→        errors.extend(visible_errors)
   292→        if remaining > 0:
   293→            errors.append(f"... {remaining} additional schema error(s) omitted")
   294→        return None, errors
   295→
   296→    return issues_data, []
   297→
   298→
   299→def load_import_issues_data(
   300→    import_file: str,
   301→    *,
   302→    config: ImportLoadConfig | None = None,
   303→    colorize_fn=None,
   304→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   305→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   306→    trusted_assessment_source: bool = False,
   307→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None = None,
   308→    attested_external: bool = False,
   309→    manual_override: bool = False,
   310→    manual_attest: str | None = None,
   311→) -> ReviewImportPayload:
   312→    """Load and normalize review import payload to object format.
   313→
   314→    Raises ``ImportPayloadLoadError`` when validation fails.
   315→    """
   316→    _ = colorize_fn
   317→    conflict_errors = _config_and_legacy_kwargs_conflict(
   318→        config=config,
   319→        lang_name=lang_name,
   320→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   321→        trusted_assessment_source=trusted_assessment_source,
   322→        trusted_assessment_label=trusted_assessment_label,
   323→        attested_external=attested_external,
   324→        manual_override=manual_override,
   325→        manual_attest=manual_attest,
   326→    )
   327→    if conflict_errors:
   328→        raise ImportPayloadLoadError(conflict_errors)
   329→    options = config or ImportLoadConfig(
   330→        lang_name=lang_name,
   331→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   332→        trusted_assessment_source=trusted_assessment_source,
   333→        trusted_assessment_label=trusted_assessment_label,
   334→        attested_external=attested_external,
   335→        manual_override=manual_override,
   336→        manual_attest=manual_attest,
   337→    )
   338→    data, errors = _parse_and_validate_import(
   339→        import_file,
   340→        config=options,
   341→    )
   342→    if errors:
   343→        raise ImportPayloadLoadError(errors)
   344→    if data is None:
   345→        raise ValueError(
   346→            "import payload missing after parse completed without validation errors"
   347→        )
   348→    return data
   349→
   350→
   351→def print_assessment_policy_notice(
   352→    policy,
   353→    *,
   354→    import_file: str,
   355→    colorize_fn,
   356→) -> None:
   357→    """Render trust/override status for assessment-bearing imports."""
   358→    policy_model = AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(policy)
   359→    if not policy_model.assessments_present:
   360→        return
   361→    mode = policy_model.mode.strip().lower()
   362→    reason = policy_model.reason.strip()
   363→
   364→    if mode == "trusted":
   365→        packet_path = policy_model.provenance.packet_path.strip() or None
   366→        detail = f" · blind packet {packet_path}" if packet_path else ""
   367→        print(
   368→            colorize_fn(
   369→                f"  Assessment provenance: trusted blind batch artifact{detail}.",
   370→                "dim",
   371→            )
   372→        )
   373→        return
   374→
   375→    if mode == "trusted_internal":
   376→        count = int(policy_model.assessment_count or 0)
   377→        reason_text = policy_model.reason.strip()
   378→        suffix = f" ({reason_text})" if reason_text else ""
   379→        print(
   380→            colorize_fn(
   381→                f"  Assessment updates applied: {count} dimension(s){suffix}.",
   382→                "dim",
   383→            )
   384→        )
   385→        return
   386→
   387→    if mode == "manual_override":
   388→        count = int(policy_model.assessment_count or 0)
   389→        print(
   390→            colorize_fn(
   391→                f"  WARNING: applying {count} assessment update(s) via manual override from untrusted provenance.",
   392→                "yellow",
   393→            )
   394→        )
   395→        if reason:
   396→            print(colorize_fn(f"  Reason: {reason}", "dim"))
   397→        return
   398→
   399→    if mode == "attested_external":
   400→        count = int(policy_model.assessment_count or 0)
   401→        print(
   402→            colorize_fn(
   403→                f"  Assessment updates applied via attested external blind review: {count} dimension(s).",
   404→                "dim",
   405→            )
   406→        )
   407→        if reason:
   408→            print(colorize_fn(f"  Reason: {reason}", "dim"))
   409→        return
   410→
   411→    if mode == "issues_only":
   412→        count = int(policy_model.assessment_count or 0)
   413→        print(
   414→            colorize_fn(
   415→                "  WARNING: untrusted assessment source detected. "
   416→                f"Imported issues only; skipped {count} assessment score update(s).",
   417→                "yellow",
   418→            )
   419→        )
   420→        if reason:
   421→            print(colorize_fn(f"  Reason: {reason}", "dim"))
   422→        print(
   423→            colorize_fn(
   424→                "  Assessment scores in state were left unchanged.",
   425→                "dim",
   426→            )
   427→        )
   428→        print(
   429→            colorize_fn(
   430→                "  Happy path: use `desloppify review --run-batches --parallel --scan-after-import`.",
   431→                "dim",
   432→            )
   433→        )
   434→        print(
   435→            colorize_fn(
   436→                "  If you intentionally want manual assessment import, rerun with "
   437→                f"`desloppify review --import {import_file} --manual-override --attest \"<why this is justified>\"`.",
   438→                "dim",
   439→            )
   440→        )
   441→        print(
   442→            colorize_fn(
   443→                "  Claude cloud path for durable scores: "
   444→                f"`desloppify review --import {import_file} --attested-external "
   445→                f"--attest \"{ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_ATTEST_EXAMPLE}\"`",
   446→                "dim",
   447→            )
   448→        )
   449→
   450→
   451→__all__ = [
   452→    "ImportLoadConfig",
   453→    "ImportPayloadLoadError",
   454→    "assessment_mode_label",
   455→    "assessment_policy_model_from_payload",
   456→    "assessment_policy_from_payload",
   457→    "load_import_issues_data",
   458→    "print_assessment_mode_banner",
   459→    "print_import_load_errors",
   460→    "print_assessment_policy_notice",
   461→    "print_assessments_summary",
   462→    "print_open_review_summary",
   463→    "print_review_import_scores_and_integrity",
   464→    "print_skipped_validation_details",
   465→    "resolve_override_context",
   466→]
   467→

    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→# Helpers

    14→
    15→def _state_with_review_issues(*ids: str) -> dict:
    16→    """Build minimal state with open review issues."""
    17→    issues = {}
    18→    for fid in ids:
    19→        issues[fid] = {
    20→            "status": "open",
    21→            "detector": "review",
    22→            "file": "test.py",
    23→            "summary": f"Review issue {fid}",
    24→            "confidence": "medium",
    25→            "tier": 2,
    26→            "detail": {"dimension": "abstraction_fitness"},
    27→        }
    28→    return {"issues": issues, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    29→
    30→
    31→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    32→    """Build a plan with triage stages pre-recorded."""
    33→    plan = empty_plan()
    34→    plan["queue_order"] = list(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS)
    35→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
    36→    stages = meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
    37→    for name in stage_names:
    38→        stages[name] = {
    39→            "stage": name,
    40→            "report": f"A sufficiently long report for {name} stage that meets minimum length requirements and more text",
    41→            "cited_ids": ["r1", "r2", "r3"],
    42→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    43→            "issue_count": 5,
    44→        }
    45→        if confirmed:
    46→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    47→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = "I have thoroughly reviewed all the issues in this stage"
    48→    return plan
    49→
    50→
    51→def _fake_runtime(state: dict):
    52→    """Build a minimal command_runtime return."""
    53→    return type("Ctx", (), {"state": state, "config": {}})()
    54→
    55→
    56→def _fake_args(**overrides) -> argparse.Namespace:
    57→    """Build an argparse.Namespace with default triage args."""
    58→    defaults = {
    59→        "lang": None,
    60→        "path": ".",
    61→        "confirm": None,
    62→        "attestation": None,
    63→        "confirmed": None,
    64→        "stage": None,

    67→        "confirm_existing": False,
    68→        "strategy": None,
    69→        "note": None,
    70→        "start": False,
    71→        "dry_run": False,
    72→    }
    73→    defaults.update(overrides)
    74→    return argparse.Namespace(**defaults)

    81→class TestConfirmObserve:
    82→    def test_confirm_observe_shows_summary_without_attestation(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
    83→        """Without --attestation, confirm observe shows summary and guidance."""
    84→        plan = _plan_with_stages("observe")
    85→        state = _state_with_review_issues("r1", "r2", "r3")
    86→
    87→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
    88→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "command_runtime", lambda args: _fake_runtime(state))
    89→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
    90→
    91→        args = _fake_args(confirm="observe")

    96→        # Should NOT have confirmed
    97→        assert "confirmed_at" not in plan["epic_triage_meta"]["triage_stages"]["observe"]

    99→    def test_confirm_observe_attestation_too_short(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
   100→        """Attestation shorter than 30 chars is rejected."""

   106→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)

   114→    def test_confirm_observe_records_confirmation(self, monkeypatch, capsys):

    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→# Helpers

    14→
    15→def _state_with_issues(*ids: str, dimension: str = "naming") -> dict:
    16→    issues = {}
    17→    for fid in ids:
    18→        issues[fid] = {
    19→            "status": "open",
    20→            "detector": "review",
    21→            "file": "test.py",
    22→            "summary": f"Review issue {fid}",
    23→            "confidence": "medium",
    24→            "tier": 2,
    25→            "detail": {"dimension": dimension},
    26→        }
    27→    return {"issues": issues, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    28→
    29→
    30→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    31→    plan = empty_plan()
    32→    plan["queue_order"] = list(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS)
    33→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
    34→    stages = meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
    35→    for name in stage_names:
    36→        stages[name] = {
    37→            "stage": name,
    38→            "report": (
    39→                "A sufficiently long report for the stage that meets minimum "
    40→                "length requirements and more text to ensure validation passes"
    41→            ),
    42→            "cited_ids": ["r1", "r2", "r3"],
    43→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    44→            "issue_count": 5,
    45→        }
    46→        if confirmed:
    47→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    48→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = (
    49→                "I have thoroughly reviewed all the issues in naming dimension "
    50→                "and this stage analysis is complete"
    51→            )
    52→    return plan
    53→
    54→
    55→def _plan_with_enriched_clusters(stage_names, confirmed=False):
    56→    """Plan with stages + enriched manual clusters (for organize tests)."""
    57→    plan = _plan_with_stages(*stage_names, confirmed=confirmed)
    58→    plan["clusters"]["fix-naming"] = {
    59→        "name": "fix-naming",
    60→        "issue_ids": ["r1", "r2", "r3", "r4", "r5"],
    61→        "description": "Fix naming issues",
    62→        "action_steps": ["step 1", "step 2", "step 3", "step 4", "step 5"],
    63→        "auto": False,
    64→        "user_modified": True,
    65→        "created_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    66→        "updated_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    67→        "cluster_key": "",
    68→        "action": None,
    69→    }
    70→    return plan
    71→
    72→
    73→def _fake_runtime(state: dict):
    74→    return type("Ctx", (), {"state": state, "config": {}})()
    75→
    76→
    77→def _fake_args(**overrides) -> argparse.Namespace:
    78→    defaults = {
    79→        "lang": None,
    80→        "path": ".",
    81→        "confirm": None,
    82→        "attestation": None,
    83→        "confirmed": None,
    84→        "stage": None,

    89→        "note": None,
    90→        "start": False,
    91→        "dry_run": False,
    92→    }
    93→    defaults.update(overrides)

    10→
    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→# Helpers
    13→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14→
    15→def _state_with_issues(*ids: str, dimension: str = "naming") -> dict:
    16→    issues = {}
    17→    for fid in ids:
    18→        issues[fid] = {
    19→            "status": "open",
    20→            "detector": "review",
    21→            "file": "test.py",
    22→            "summary": f"Review issue {fid}",
    23→            "confidence": "medium",
    24→            "tier": 2,
    25→            "detail": {"dimension": dimension},
    26→        }
    27→    return {"issues": issues, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    28→
    29→
    30→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    31→    plan = empty_plan()
    32→    plan["queue_order"] = list(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS)
    33→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
    34→    stages = meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
    35→    for name in stage_names:
    36→        stages[name] = {
    37→            "stage": name,
    38→            "report": (
    39→                "A sufficiently long report for the stage that meets minimum "
    40→                "length requirements and more text to ensure validation passes"
    41→            ),
    42→            "cited_ids": ["r1", "r2", "r3"],
    43→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    44→            "issue_count": 5,
    45→        }
    46→        if confirmed:
    47→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    48→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = (
    49→                "I have thoroughly reviewed all the issues in naming dimension "
    50→                "and this stage analysis is complete"
    51→            )
    52→    return plan
    53→
    54→
    55→def _plan_with_enriched_clusters(stage_names, confirmed=False):
    56→    """Plan with stages + enriched manual clusters."""
    57→    plan = _plan_with_stages(*stage_names, confirmed=confirmed)
    58→    plan["clusters"]["fix-naming"] = {
    59→        "name": "fix-naming",
    60→        "issue_ids": ["r1", "r2", "r3", "r4", "r5"],
    61→        "description": "Fix naming issues",
    62→        "action_steps": ["step 1", "step 2", "step 3", "step 4", "step 5"],
    63→        "auto": False,
    64→        "user_modified": True,
    65→        "created_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    66→        "updated_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    67→        "cluster_key": "",
    68→        "action": None,
    69→    }
    70→    return plan
    71→
    72→
    73→def _fake_runtime(state: dict):
    74→    return type("Ctx", (), {"state": state, "config": {}})()
    75→
    76→
    77→def _fake_args(**overrides) -> argparse.Namespace:
    78→    defaults = {
    79→        "lang": None,
    80→        "path": ".",
    81→        "confirm": None,
    82→        "attestation": None,
    83→        "confirmed": None,
    84→        "stage": None,

    90→        "start": False,
    91→        "dry_run": False,
    92→    }
    93→    defaults.update(overrides)
    94→    return argparse.Namespace(**defaults)

   320→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
   321→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "command_runtime", lambda args: _fake_runtime(state))
   322→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
   323→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "save_plan", lambda p: None)
   324→
   325→        # Jump back to reflect with new report — clears organize confirmation
   326→        new_report = (
   327→            "Revised strategy: after further analysis, the naming issues "
   328→            "have a deeper root cause than initially thought. Need to "
   329→            "restructure approach to address the root cause first."
   330→        )
   331→        args = _fake_args(stage="reflect", report=new_report)
   332→        triage_mod.cmd_plan_triage(args)
   333→        capsys.readouterr()  # discard first output
   334→
   335→        # Now run triage with no args to see the dashboard
   336→        args2 = _fake_args()
   337→        triage_mod.cmd_plan_triage(args2)
   338→        out = capsys.readouterr().out
   339→
   340→        assert "needs confirm" in out
   341→

     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→import desloppify.app.commands.plan.triage_handlers as triage_mod
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.constants import TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS
    10→
    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→# Helpers
    13→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14→
    15→def _state_with_review_issues(*ids: str) -> dict:
    16→    issues = {}
    17→    for fid in ids:
    18→        issues[fid] = {
    19→            "status": "open",
    20→            "detector": "review",
    21→            "file": "test.py",
    22→            "summary": f"Review issue {fid}",
    23→            "confidence": "medium",
    24→            "tier": 2,
    25→            "detail": {"dimension": "abstraction_fitness"},
    26→        }
    27→    return {"issues": issues, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    28→
    29→
    30→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    31→    plan = empty_plan()
    32→    plan["queue_order"] = list(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS)
    33→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
    34→    stages = meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
    35→    for name in stage_names:
    36→        stages[name] = {
    37→            "stage": name,
    38→            "report": f"A sufficiently long report for {name} stage that meets minimum length requirements and more text",
    39→            "cited_ids": ["r1", "r2", "r3"],
    40→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    41→            "issue_count": 5,
    42→        }
    43→        if confirmed:
    44→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    45→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = "I have thoroughly reviewed all the issues in this stage"
    46→    return plan
    47→
    48→
    49→def _fake_runtime(state: dict):
    50→    return type("Ctx", (), {"state": state, "config": {}})()
    51→
    52→
    53→def _fake_args(**overrides) -> argparse.Namespace:
    54→    defaults = {
    55→        "lang": None,
    56→        "path": ".",
    57→        "confirm": None,
    58→        "attestation": None,
    59→        "confirmed": None,
    60→        "stage": None,
    61→        "report": None,
    62→        "complete": False,
    63→        "confirm_existing": False,
    64→        "strategy": None,
    65→        "note": None,
    66→        "start": False,
    67→        "dry_run": False,
    68→    }
    69→    defaults.update(overrides)
    70→    return argparse.Namespace(**defaults)
    71→
    72→
    73→def _log_actions(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
    74→    """Extract action names from execution log."""
    75→    return [e.get("action", "") for e in plan.get("execution_log", [])]
    76→
    77→
    78→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    79→# Observe stage logs entry
    80→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    81→
    82→class TestObserveLogging:
    83→    def test_observe_stage_logs_entry(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
    84→        plan = _plan_with_stages()
    85→        state = _state_with_review_issues("r1", "r2")
    86→
    87→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
    88→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "command_runtime", lambda args: _fake_runtime(state))
    89→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "save_plan", lambda p, *a, **kw: None)
    90→        monkeypatch.setattr(triage_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
    91→
    92→        report = "A sufficiently long analysis of themes and root causes across the codebase with contradictions noted"
    93→        args = _fake_args(stage="observe", report=report)
    94→        triage_mod.cmd_plan_triage(args)
    95→        assert "triage_observe" in _log_actions(plan)
    96→

     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

    24→
    25→def _render_import_load_error(
    26→    exc: ImportPayloadLoadError,
    27→    *,
    28→    import_file: Path | str,

    31→    print_import_load_errors(

    35→    )
    36→    return capsys.readouterr().err

    39→def test_assessment_mode_label_mappings():
    40→    assert assessment_mode_label({"mode": "trusted_internal"}) == (
    41→        "trusted internal (durable scores)"
    42→    )
    43→    assert assessment_mode_label({"mode": "attested_external"}) == (
    44→        "attested external (durable scores)"
    45→    )
    46→    assert assessment_mode_label({"mode": "manual_override"}) == (
    47→        "manual override (provisional scores)"
    48→    )
    49→    assert assessment_mode_label({"mode": "issues_only"}) == (
    50→        "issues-only (assessments skipped)"
    51→    )

    57→        colorize_fn=_colorize,

    59→    out = capsys.readouterr().out

    77→    assert policy["mode"] == "issues_only"

Now I can see the pattern. The failing tests have report strings that don't contain the issue IDs (like "r1", "r2", etc.). The reports are generic strings like "A sufficiently long report about strategy and comparing issues against completed work and more text" — none of them mention "r1", "r2", etc.

The tests need their report strings updated to include the issue IDs. Let me check exactly which tests fail and what reports they use.

Looking at the 9 failures:

1. **test_triage_confirmation.py:184** - `test_reflect_proceeds_with_confirmed_observe` - report doesn't contain r1, r2
2. **test_triage_confirmation.py:253** - `test_organize_blocked_without_confirmed_reflect` - report doesn't contain r1, r2
3. **test_triage_fold_confirm.py:132** - `test_reflect_auto_confirms_observe_with_attestation` - reflect_report doesn't contain r1-r5
4. **test_triage_fold_confirm.py:232** - `test_organize_auto_confirms_reflect_with_attestation` - organize_report doesn't contain r1-r5
5. **test_triage_jump_back.py:124** - `test_rerun_reflect_with_new_report_clears_organize_confirmation` - new_report doesn't contain r1-r5
6. **test_triage_jump_back.py:148** - `test_rerun_reflect_without_report_reuses_data` - uses stored report (from `_plan_with_stages`) which doesn't contain r1-r5
7. **test_triage_jump_back.py:225** - `test_jump_back_then_fold_confirm_forward` - Step 1 uses stored report, which doesn't contain r1-r5
8. **test_triage_jump_back.py:340** - `test_stage_progress_shows_needs_confirm` - new_report doesn't contain r1-r5
9. **test_triage_logging.py:135** - `test_reflect_stage_logs_entry` - report doesn't contain r1, r2

The issue is that the reflect stage validation checks that the report accounts for all open review issues. The stored reports and new reports in these tests don't mention the issue IDs.

The cleanest fix: update each test's report string to include the issue IDs. But first let me check how the `_plan_with_stages` helper creates reports — many tests reuse stored reports from there.

For `_plan_with_stages` in each file, the stored report is generic and doesn't include issue IDs. When reflect reuses its stored report, validation still fails. I need to update:

1. The `_plan_with_stages` helpers in all 4 files to include issue IDs in stored reports
2. The explicit report strings in failing tests

Let me also check which tests have 5 issues vs 2-3 to match correctly. Let me update the `_plan_with_stages` helpers and explicit report strings.

For `_plan_with_stages` - the `cited_ids` are always `["r1", "r2", "r3"]`, so mentioning those in the report. But some tests create state with 5 issues (r1-r5) or 2 issues (r1, r2). The issue accounting checks against valid_ids from state, not cited_ids.

Let me trace the logic more carefully. Let me look at what `valid_ids` is passed to the validation function.

So `valid_ids` comes from `si.open_issues.keys()` — which are the state issue IDs. In the tests, these are the IDs passed to `_state_with_issues` or `_state_with_review_issues` (e.g., "r1", "r2" or "r1"..."r5").

The fix needs the report to mention all these issue IDs. The simplest approach: update the test report strings to include the issue IDs. Let me update all 4 test files.

For **test_triage_confirmation.py**:
- `_plan_with_stages` report: add " r1 r2 r3" (since cited_ids are r1,r2,r3)
- Line 164/180: reports for reflect with state("r1","r2") — need r1, r2 in report
- Line 249: report for organize with state("r1","r2") — need r1, r2 in report

For **test_triage_fold_confirm.py**:
- `_plan_with_stages` report: need to contain issue IDs
- Line 117-119: reflect_report needs r1-r5
- Line 219-222: organize_report needs r1-r5

For **test_triage_jump_back.py**:
- `_plan_with_stages` report: needs r1-r5 (most tests use 5 issues)
- Line 114-117: new_report needs r1-r5
- Line 326-329: new_report needs r1-r5

For **test_triage_logging.py**:
- `_plan_with_stages` report: similar
- Line 132: report needs r1, r2

The simplest, most robust fix: update `_plan_with_stages` in each file to include the cited_ids in the report text, and update each explicit report string to mention the relevant issue IDs.

Wait, the organize stage doesn't go through reflect issue accounting — that's only for the reflect stage. Let me re-check what the test failure was about. Looking back at the error:

For `test_jump_back_then_fold_confirm_forward` (line 225):
```
assert stages["organize"].get("confirmed_at") is None
E   AssertionError: assert '2025-06-01T00:01:00Z' is None
```

This is in Step 1 (line 219-225), where it runs `--stage reflect` with no report (reuse mode). The test expects that organize's confirmation gets cleared (cascade). But the reflect stage validation is failing (report doesn't mention issue IDs), so the stage isn't being recorded at all, so the cascade doesn't happen, so organize's confirmed_at remains.

With my fix to include issue IDs in the stored report, the reflect reuse should now succeed, and the cascade should clear organize's confirmation.
